# Decisions

Every product, design and technical call made while building, newest first. "Owner" means the owner decided;
"Claude" means Claude took its own recommendation under the standing permission of 23 Nov 2026 (reversible
calls only). Anything about money, legal, launch dates or customers goes to the owner.

| Date | Decision | Why | By | Undo by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 26 Nov 2026 | Item 78c: the phone keeps its pairing in IndexedDB (`manut-mac`): the host id, its device id, the relay door token, K as an HKDF key made with `extractable: false`, the passkey's id and relying party, and the door ids of the last 32 codes it started. This is the one exception to the web app's "nothing kept" rule; nothing of a chat is ever kept | A pairing that a reload forgets is no pairing, and a key that script cannot read back is what the design asked for. The door token must be text because the relay's hello carries it, and it opens only the relay's door | Claude | Delete the database: every phone pairs again |
| 26 Nov 2026 | Item 78c: a code's door is written to the vault as spent before the relay is dialled, and a spent door is refused before anything is sent, also after a reload, in a second tab and after Forget this Mac | The package refuses a second start only within one page; the README asks the app to hold the one try across reloads | Claude | None advised: a phone that retried would let a relay holding a photographed code play the Mac until the digits matched |
| 26 Nov 2026 | Item 78c: the passkey is made from a tap on "Make a passkey" once the Mac took the digits, and Allow once tries the prompt at once and falls back to a Confirm button when the browser refuses a prompt that did not come from a tap | Safari opens a passkey prompt only from the person's tap; the Mac's answer arrives later over the relay, so a prompt opened then may be refused | Claude | Open the prompt only from Confirm |
| 26 Nov 2026 | Item 78c: an answer to a question goes through the same challenge and passkey path as Allow once (`src/app/mac/decision.ts`) whenever the Mac sends a challenge for it; today's Mac takes it at once | The lead's note from item 115's security review: a stolen unlocked phone could answer questions with no Face ID. The digest the challenge binds and the field of step two are to be matched to item 115's final shape | Claude | Item 115 decides the wire; the phone follows it in one file |
| 26 Nov 2026 | Item 78c: the Your Mac tab shows in the nav before signing in (Today and Your Mac), since it needs no sign-in, and says "not available yet" while `/v1/health` says the relay is off or the page is not on the API's own origin | Signing in by email is off in every real environment, so the tab would otherwise be unreachable; the passkey and the relay are tied to the API's exact host | Claude | Show it only to signed-in people |
| 26 Nov 2026 | Item 78c: a pairing link opened outside the installed app pairs nothing, is taken out of the address at once, and the page offers Copy link to paste inside the app; one opened inside the installed app fills the field and waits for Pair | On an iPhone a link from the camera lands in Safari, whose storage the Home Screen app does not share, and the code's secret should stay in no history entry | Claude | Pair from the browser too |

A call is priced first, its worst case is held against the shop's monthly cap in the shop's TenantObject (in one synchronous transaction, 402 `budget_exceeded` past the cap, reaching the cap exactly allowed), then settled at the provider's token counts. A provider failure releases the hold; an answer whose usage cannot be read is charged the worst case; a hold nothing settles in 15 minutes is charged as abandoned. Spend is kept in millisatang with BigInt and rounded up once per call; months are Bangkok months. Prices per million tokens: Haiku 5.5 $0.10 in and $0.50 out ($0.50 and $2.50 past 100K prompt tokens), Sonnet 5.5 $2 and $10, Opus 5.5 $4 and $20; any other model is refused. The defaults are the owner's to change: ฿35.00 a dollar and ฿200 per shop per month. No HTTP route calls a model; workers call `callModel` in code.
